## Run C1 — §7.1, antitonic fits (Lemmas 7.1–7.3)

- **Sent:** 6 Oct 2026, ~16:30. Status at sending: queued.
- **Input:** `lean/C1_antitonic_fits.md` (md5 `9cefddf90b44e752732c2f6c1b70a638`). Instruction: `lean/instrC1.txt` (md5 `110f045b3903d5da9e63b788d2ea23e3`). New folder asked: `RequestProject/AntitonicFit/` (namespace `AntitonicFit`).
- **Brute force before sending:** `lean/chkC1.py` (md5 `0a3d9a885434ad9df307e05c62cdd485`), log `logs/chkC1.log`: 0 failures; both controls fire. Two errors of my own script were found on the way and are published: `logs/chkC1_first_run_my_extra_check.log` (I had added «the integer fit is non-increasing», which the paper does not claim and is false) and `logs/chkC1_second_run_my_typo.log` (a mistyped `x + δ`).
- **To audit when it finishes:**
  - download the project and read `lean-toolchain` and `lake-manifest.json`;
  - grep for `sorry`, `admit`, `axiom`, `native_decide`, `extern`;
  - compile locally under the Lean cap, one module at a time;
  - `#check` and `#print axioms` of every statement;
  - check faithfulness. Read with care: (a) `fit` is the minimizer, not an algorithm; (b) the integer fit puts the ceilings FIRST and uses the non-negative remainder; (c) «cut» is the equality of `fit(x)` with the concatenation, and «strict» adds the strict drop; (d) (b2) carries «δ constant on every level set of fit(x)» and integer `x`, `δ`; (e) the example (3,4,3,4) is stated as three facts; (f) Lemma 7.3 has `max` on the first half and `min` on the second.

## Run C40b — the formal dance of payment systems: Lemma 10.6

- **Sent:** 2026-10-09 09:45:16 (BITACORA, «AFTER send C40b»), task `afeddeb6-2004-49ea-b54d-237d18561b66`, by the JS click on `button[aria-label="Solve"]` (instruction length 2 229 characters, checked against the file). Folder asked `RequestProject/FormalDance/`, namespace `FormalDance`.

Systems are matrices over Bf; agreement x ~ y on the floors f ≥ m (the values that act) is a congruence and does not change the action under (top W); a clean move is P invertible up to agreement and Dd − B P⁻¹ C ~ 2G'; the dance is a relation by recursion on ds, with the relabelling of C13 on split steps. Lemma 10.6: the twist move of a payment system has P = 1 and S = 2·paySys(pT, κT) exactly; the split move has P = 1 and S ~ 2·paySys(pSp, κSp); hence the formal dance of a payment system IS the data dance of C13, and its final matrix is Mfin (for σ, the matrix of Theorem 4.4 by C14); uniqueness without 2-torsion; scaling by a unit.

Only agreement is claimed.
